orchestration\tasks\TASK-0031-browser-d106-audit\REPORT.md:23:`entombFallenScion` ([`server/core/services/chronicles.js:206-218`](../../../server/core/services/chronicles.js#L206-L218), [`server/core/repositories/chronicles-repository.js:467-505`](../../../server/core/repositories/chronicles-repository.js#L467-L505)). The later return handler's `removeItemIdentity` is a separate JSON-Store path: it is guarded by `record.exists` and only runs when that JSON `entomb` returns a relic ([`server/player/handlers/socket-events/index.js:462-482`](../../../server/player/handlers/socket-events/index.js#L462-L482)). It is therefore incorrect to say the selected relic is universally removed only on return. In either path the dead player is then removed from the world and a successor is created from the fresh Scion profile ([`server/player/handlers/socket-events/index.js:484-507`](../../../server/player/handlers/socket-events/index.js#L484-L507), [`server/core/entities/player/fresh-scion-profile.js:21-46`](../../../server/core/entities/player/fresh-scion-profile.js#L21-L46)). Thus remaining carried state disappears with the dead in-memory player. D-106 requires every carried item and trophy to remain recoverable, so the hard path is a direct product violation.
